The speaking time of the Presidents of the Parliamentary Groups will be laid down in Rule 97(2) and (3) of the Rules of Procedure, i.e. the speaking time of the Prime Minister and of the Chief of Staff of Officers will be up to 20 minutes and the Presidents of the other six Parliamentary Groups up to 15 minutes. The ministers and the Deputy Ministers will speak for ten minutes an hour. As long as there is a chairman of the parliamentary group who does not wish to speak, the floor will be given by the Deputy parliamentary representative for 12 minutes. At the end of the debate and pursuant to Rules 73 and 157, paragraph 3 of the Rules of Procedure, the Plenary Assembly shall decide by secret ballot on the committee's proposal and separately on any alleged act or omission for which a prosecution is sought, as defined and specified in that committee's finding, in accordance with the provisions of Rules 86 of the Constitution, 153 et seq. of the Rules of Procedure of the Parliament and of Law 31268/2003, the Criminal Responsibility of the Ministers as they apply. The decision in question is taken by an absolute majority of the total number of Members, i.e. by a hundred and fifty and one by one, as defined in the Constitution and the Rules of Parliament. Under Rule 155(8) of the Rules of Procedure, those of the Members referred to in the proposal will not vote on the proposal to prosecute them. The vote will take place at one reading of the list. It will be self-evident for each person and will be held in ten separate votes. There will be no name on the voter, only a number will be entered. The voters shall be placed on the basis of the alphabetical order of the name of the persons against whom the proposal is directed. For the purposes of the secret ballot, you will be distributed ten lists by the name of Mr. Avramopoulos, Mr. Venizelos, Mr. Georgiadis, Mr. Koutromanis, Mr. Loverdou, Mr. Lykourenzos, Mr. Pikrammenos, Mr. Salmas, Mr. Samaras and Mr. Stournaras. The vote on or not to prosecute the persons mentioned above shall be expressed by a cross under one of the three words: 'yes', 'no, because of the lack of competence of Parliament and of the transmission to the competent prosecutor' and 'the present'. I would also like to inform the House here that fellow Members or members of the government who are on a mission of Parliament and the government abroad will vote by letter, in accordance with Rule 70A of the Rules of Procedure of the House. In this case, before the vote, their votes will be delivered to the Bureau in sealed files, showing the number of ballot intended. This printed file will be enclosed in a larger second file, also closed, which will also list the name of the Member or the member of the Government, who will vote by written vote. The dossier will be accompanied by a transfer document addressed to the President of Parliament. The lists will be delivered by accredited colleagues from the MPs or members of the government who are to vote under Rule 70A of Parliament's Rules of Procedure. ParlaMint-GR_2018-05-18-S1-commons.u163	On such interesting legal issues that have been raised, I would ask you, Madam President, to give me two - three more minutes. It's really a temptation for someone legal to express their opinion. Of course, I would like to tell you that the incidental issue of constitutionality could not, in my opinion, be raised either as an issue of unconstitutionality or as an incident, because the whole debate today, from the first minute to the last - at midnight, when the end comes - relates to Article 86 of the Constitution. So from morning to night anyway, the Constitution will be discussed. It's not a big deal. It comes at the heart of the matter whether everything has been done under the Constitution or not. Nor is it a question of constitutionality in particular. What was typical of this debate, as has been done so far, is that the law appears to be a dark art, like an alchemy, and suddenly the alpha or a beta result has emerged, because it is not regarded as case law, for example, as what a preliminary review committee says, as what is or is not case-law, which defines a provision of law and one is correct and the other is completely wrong. Of course, here we are dealing with a crisis which will also be a judicial crisis, that is, previous legal crises, usually called 'legality'. A legal crisis is also made by the preliminary committee, by the preliminary examination committee and, of course, by a case-law. What leads me to revive the series of thoughts in my report and to refer first of all to legal matters, is that the whole debate on this issue, which has some very worthy of attention and from a legal point of view, has been made as an attempt to counteract novelties and ideologies of things, which are clearly and legally and socially obvious. What was said here at the beginning of these debates? 'No scandal, no designing'. But the scandal arises from indicators, economic indicators, statistical indicators, Greek, national. It's known to every citizen who goes to the hospital. How is there no scandal? All right, we don't say'management', not'scandal', we say'scandal and construction'. We're moving forward. Why was the lack of competence considered at the beginning? It had to be looked at at the end. Why not consider it at first? A matter of an institution's competence must not be known at the outset, so that we can know whether or not we are talking about an issue? Can we carry out witness examinations, any persons involved for one year and then say 'a, you know, we were unresponsible'? Of course not. There was no matter here where there was no message, just to say it. First of all, all the discussions of the committee took place under a constant request to examine witnesses and thus, through the statements, what the reality is in this case, what the responsibilities and what the guilts are. But if witnesses were to be examined, the House would have accepted its own competence to judge the act, and we would have been led to Parliament's competence and thus to waive it. What was the opposition demanded during the Preliminary Committee's work? It was constantly demanding that the House should respect its powers rather than the Constitution, so that the acts should be recorded. In other words, it called for the "decoration" of cases. This was the centre of gravity of the arguments of the Opposition, as well as all the others: Ten polls. “Why ten polls? One call." If we had one call, they would say: “Why a call? Article 60, expression of opinion. Ten to be the polls.' "Is that how the ballot is written? Why would it be like this? Otherwise the ballot will be written.’ Would we have it differently? They would ask for another wording then. A struggling effort was unfolded through legal arrangements to prevent the case from being brought to court and to see who is innocent and who is guilty. However, I now go to the very subject of today's debate in the House. Famous corruption has led to social demands of investigation, and these have either had very distinct right-wing characteristics, of the type of politicians sitting indiscriminately on the stool and of being convicted, and of taking heads, etc., whether they were democratic demands, convicted of cases, of light and of who is to blame and who is not. What does the recent history of our country show? Were we really cleared up? We had justice? No, of course not. The recent history of the country shows us that the responsibilities of politicians in most cases were left aside and there were no trials, there was no justice. What's that for? It is because Article 86 exists in the Constitution, which, in order to bring together various interests - one is security, to punish the crimes of politicians, the other is to see that there is a flow in political life and that politicians are not eternally and eternally suspected of various illegitimate acts - says that some of the actions, if carried out in the exercise of their duties, will fall within the competence of the House, if not, of course, they must go to the ordinary courts and be tried, as is being tried by each citizen. Our point was, then, that the actions we are discussing, the infidelity, bribery and laundry have been carried out in the performance of tasks, as Article 86 says. You know, at this point, Article 86 of the Constitution is not so unreasonable. What's he saying? He says: I understand that a politician at the time of his duties and taking decisions can lead to some legal acrobatics, can lead to some decisions that will even cause violent reactions, etc. and find out, because of the exercise of power, the need to make some such choices. Illegal? Of course, illegality. However, since these acts have to be carried out by the politician because he is carrying out certain tasks that he is told or will do so, and in these cases that the Constitution will be more enthusiastic to see, to go to the rules laid down in Article 86, to waive the limitations and to ensure that Parliament in some cases, by means of special procedures, remains unresponsible for the cases in question. But here we are dealing with acts which have not been done in the performance of tasks. Obviously, neither a seduction is an act carried out in the performance of the duties to go to the Constitution, nor even an act of corruption is an act of duties, so as to be subject to the Constitution and to have a short limitation. It is therefore clear - if we look at the list of acts for which they have been charged - that the act of infidelity is an act of management, which is done in the performance of certain tasks, and that is why it is 'in the performance of the acts referred to in Article 86'. Therefore, it has definitely expired. Also, an act of money laundering is obviously an act that is done not at the time of the exercise of the functions, but then, and so it is clear, that under Rule 86 we do not have the power of Parliament, but of the ordinary courts. In terms of bribery, what is the result? This was the question, because we had a precedent for the Chochatzopoulou case, which said that bribery is an act that is carried out in the performance of the duties. This position, which also emerged from the Chochazopoulou case, is not reasonable, because it is not reasonable to say that bribery is a duty and falls to carry out a duty to receive gifts. So, what is so reasonable to say that it does not belong to Article 86, but to the common courts, that the Committee on Examination has seen that many others have seen it, that we have reasonal support and documentation of the position that we have an act which is not done in the exercise of duty in the case of corruption. We have seen that well-known, top lawyers, the President of the Republic and other theoretical professors and judges in this House, have understood and accepted that bribery is not an act carried out in the performance of the duties, it does not fall under Article 86, it must go to court and be tried and if anyone is guilty, sentenced. On this basis, therefore, the Committee on Examination proposes to the plenary, not to infidelity, because this issue has been clarified by the first plenary, but by bribery and money laundering, that these acts should be brought to justice and be tried by it. Recognises Parliament, the committee for preliminary examination recognises the competence of justice, because there is no competence of the House, because this is not the case, that is to say, the acts have been carried out in execution. I therefore think that things, for our part, are clear and the recognition of the competence of justice, which arises from our findings, should be understood that it should not be taken as a triumph of others or as a disaster of them. It's just a victory for justice, it's a victory for the Constitution and the rule of law. It is a victory not just of a party or two parties or something else. It is a victory of society, it is a victory of its own policy, because the parliament today shows that with the Constitution in hand it is not afraid to fight corruption, send the case to Justice, which will be able to judge. Thank you very much.

But that is half the truth. The full truth is that the SYRIZA - ANEL Government has continued the specific cuts, has extended them, while on the pretext of dealing with corruption and rationalisation it is preparing a new blow to public pharmaceutical expenditure. And, of course, it is quite obvious that despite the drastic reduction in recent years, retail price imprints have been too small. In other words, the state expenditure on the medicine may be reduced, but it does not reduce the cost of medicines, so that the patient is additionally charged for his purchase. Regardless of the individual differences between governments, the direction and objective of the measures is to cut the state-of-the-art insurance costs for medicines, in the direction of reducing the so-called ‘non-wage costs’. All governments have taken measures. Each government implemented its previous measures and in turn extended them. He loaded patients with increased payments in relation to the past, even after reducing the retail price of drugs, the "positive list", as it is called. Comparatively, between 2009 and 2016, public expenditure, the State and the insurance funds pay 57.7% less, while the insured pay 43.1% more. Of course, the above should be calculated and the drugs of the so-called “negative list” that are prescribed but are not compensated for by the EEAH and the non-prescribed medicines, which in addition have recently been released and paid 100% of the patients, as well as the 10 euros that have now become status for the patients to prescribe doctors, because they consider that they are out of free visits. At the same time, the increase in the contributions of pensioners to the health sector - therefore also to medicines - to main and secondary pensions, which, for 2017, were estimated at EUR 717 million. These figures reveal that the governments which have made progress in reducing public spending did not do so to affect the large profits of the pharmaceutical industry, but to achieve the objectives of budgetary adjustment and, of course, to burden the workers. Another question: You are talking about corruption in the field of medicine and you are also making krone for its allegedly fighting when you are not touching the dominance of business groups and multinationals in this whole chain, from pharmaceutical research to the production and circulation of the medicine. For example, today clinical pharmaceutical research is funded by large private groups. They are required to connect the researcher to an academic development. From the profitable research programmes that ensure for universities, researchers are evaluated. From their participation in conferences funded by companies, doctors are evaluated. All serious management schools - as we have said before in the House - officially teach how to influence their products in the scientific community. In any case, then, we say, losers are only the people, losers are the patients, who pay increased amounts for medicines, etc. You have been afflicted for years by bureaucratic, suspensive procedures. The vote will take place at one reading of the list, be self-evident for each person and will take place in ten separate votes. There will be no name on the vote. Only one number will be entered. The voters shall be placed on the basis of the alphabetical order of the name of the persons against whom the proposal is directed. In particular: Voter number 1 concerns Mr Avramopoulos Dimitrios. Voter number 2 concerns Mr Venizelos Evangelos. Voter number 3 concerns Mr Georgiadis Spyridonas-Adoni. Voter number 4 concerns Mr Kutrumanis Georgios. Voter number 5 concerns Mr. Loverdos Andreas. Election number 6 concerns Mr. Lykourenzo Andreas. The vote will take place at 6.30 p.m. The vote will take place at 6.30 p.m. Election number 9 concerns Mr Samaras Antonio. Voter number 10 concerns Mr Stournara Ioannis. For the purpose of your secret ballot, ten ballots have been distributed with the names of Mr. Avramopoulos Dimitrios, Venizelos Evangelos, Georgiadis Spyridonos - Adonidos, Koutromani Georgiou, Loverdou Andreas, Lykourenzos Andreas, Pikrammenos Panagiotis, Salma Marios, Samara Antoniou and Stournara Ioannis. You have also been distributed a number of equal numbers of white lists, together with equal numbers of white files, to which the numbers from 1 to 10 are printed corresponding to the voters for whom they are intended. Samples of the ballots as well as the committee's distributed findings will be recorded in the minutes of today's sitting.
ParlaMint-GR_2018-05-18-S1-commons.u517	In each ballot, the number of the voter relating to each of the persons mentioned above shall be entered under the heading ‘PRODUCT’. You have also been distributed a table corresponding to the name of each person and the number of voter concerned. The vote on or not to prosecute the persons referred to above shall be expressed by a cross under one of the words ‘NEW’, ‘NOV’ on account of the lack of competence of Parliament and shall be forwarded to the competent prosecutor, or ‘PAH’. If the ballot does not bring any crosses, then I propose that it be considered invalid. Also, when counting the ballots that will be found in the wrong ballot, I propose that they be considered void. Does this House agree?
ParlaMint-GR_2018-05-18-S1-commons.u518	The House therefore agreed. In accordance with paragraph 10 of Rule 73 of the Rules of Procedure, in the event of disagreement between the number of votes cast and the number of votes cast, the count shall be repeated. If and after this new count, the number of votes cast is higher than the number of votes cast, a number of ballots shall be destroyed, before sorting, equal to the number of additional ballots. In giving the name of each Member of Parliament the vote will be taken by the voter, where he will put the file with his vote in the ballot and will confirm the vote on the vote, stating the name of each of the Members who is voting.
